\vol\colon\Sigma_{\mathscr{C}}\to\mathbb{R}
via

\vol(\tilde{\xi})=\int_{\tilde{r}\leqslant 1}\frac{\tilde{\omega}^{m}}{m!}.

for any Kähler cone metric
\tilde{\omega}=\frac{i}{2}\partial\overline{\partial}\tilde{r}^{2}
with Reeb field
\tilde{\xi}
. This is well-defined and strictly convex, hence has a unique critical point. Furthermore,
\tilde{\xi}\in\Sigma_{\mathscr{C}}
is a critical point if and only if there is a Ricci-flat Kähler cone metric with Reeb vector field
\tilde{\xi}
.

Corollary 2.35
.

The Reeb vector field
JV
for the tangent cone at infinity
\omega_{\infty}
is equal to
\xi
. Therefore, there exists an automorphism
\Psi\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
such that
\Psi^{*}\omega_{\infty}=\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
.

Proof.

Proposition
2.30
and Lemma
2.31
show that
\xi,JV\in\Sigma_{\mathscr{C}}
. Theorem
2.34
therefore proves equality as they are both associated with Ricci-flat Kähler cone metrics. As both
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
and
\omega_{\infty}
are Ricci-flat Kähler cones with the same scaling vector field and transversal holomorphic structure, the Bando-Mabuchi argument
[
4
, Theorem A]
, suitably generalized by Nitta-Sekiya
[
39
, Theorem A]
, proves the corollary.
∎

As all of the arguments above also apply to
\epsilon_{i}\to 0
in (
2.9
), i.e. for the tangent cone
\omega_{0}
at
o
, we immediately show Corollary
2.35
for
\omega_{0}
:

Corollary 2.36
.

Corollary
2.35
also applies to
\omega_{0}
, the tangent cone at o.

2.5.
Combining Tangent Cones to Liouville Theorem

The last section proved that the tangent cones at
o
resp.
\infty
of
\omega
equal
\Psi_{0}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
resp.
\Psi_{\infty}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
for automorphisms
\Psi_{0},\Psi_{\infty}\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
. Given this, an analysis of (
2.20
) yields the proof of the Liouville Theorem (Theorem
2.3
).

Proof of Theorem
2.3
.

Corollaries
2.35
and
2.36
proved that the asymptotic limits
\omega_{0}
and
\omega_{\infty}
are conical metrics and that there exists an isomorphism
\tilde{\Psi}\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
such that
\tilde{\Psi}^{*}\omega_{\infty}=\omega_{0}
. By the proof of Theorem
2.21
,
\mathcal{W}_{\epsilon}(t)=\mathcal{W}(\epsilon_{i}^{2}t)
is nonincreasing in time (recall that
\mathcal{W}_{\epsilon}(t)
is (
2.20
) for
\omega_{\epsilon}
), with
\mathcal{W}_{0}(t)=\text{constant}=\lim_{t\to 0}\mathcal{W}(t)
and
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}(t)=\text{constant}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathcal{W}(t)
. Set
\tilde{r}=\dist_{\omega_{\infty}}(o,\cdot)
and choose
c
in
f_{\infty,t}=\frac{\tilde{r}^{2}}{4t}+c
by Theorem
2.21
such that

(2.27)

\int_{\mathscr{C}}H_{\omega_{\infty}}(o,\cdot)\dvol_{\omega_{\infty}}=\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{m}}\int_{\mathscr{C}}e^{-f}\dvol_{\omega_{\infty}}=\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{m}}\int_{\mathscr{C}}e^{-\frac{\tilde{r}^{2}}{4t}-c}\dvol_{\omega_{\infty}}=1.

When
(\mathscr{C},\omega_{\infty})\cong(\mathbb{R}^{2m}\setminus\{0\},\omega_{\eucl})
, we have
c=0
. By writing
\dvol_{\omega_{\infty}}=\tilde{r}^{2m-1}d\tilde{r}\wedge\dvol_{\omega_{\infty}|_{L}}
and comparing with the Euclidean integral, we see that

c=-\log\left(\frac{\vol_{\omega_{\infty}}(L)}{\vol_{\eucl}(S^{2m-1})}\right).

The expression for
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}(t)
becomes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\infty}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathscr{C}}\left(t|\nabla_{\omega}f_{\infty,t}(o,y)|_{\omega}^{2}+f_{\infty,t}(o,y)-2m\right)u(t,y)\dvol_{\omega_{\infty}}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{m}}\int_{\mathscr{C}}\left(\frac{\tilde{r}^{2}}{4t}+\frac{\tilde{r}^{2}}{4t}-2m-c\right)e^{-\frac{\tilde{r}^{2}}{4t}-c}\dvol_{\omega_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{m}}\int_{\mathscr{C}}\left(\frac{\tilde{r}^{2}}{2t}-2m\right)e^{-\frac{\tilde{r}^{2}}{4t}-c}\dvol_{\omega_{\infty}}-\frac{c}{(4\pi t)^{m}}\int_{\mathscr{C}}e^{-\frac{\tilde{r}^{2}}{4t}-c}\dvol_{\omega_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=0+\log\left(\frac{\vol_{\omega_{\infty}}(L)}{\vol_{\eucl}(S^{2m-1})}\right).

Thus,

\lim_{t\to 0}\mathcal{W}(t)=\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathcal{W}(t)=\log\left(\frac{\vol_{\omega_{\infty}}(L)}{\vol_{\eucl}(S^{2m-1})}\right),

since
\omega_{\infty}=\tilde{\Psi}^{*}\omega_{0}
, and therefore they have the same volume for their link
L
. Since
\mathcal{W}(t)
is nonincreasing, it follows that

\mathcal{W}(t)=\log\left(\frac{\vol_{\omega_{\infty}}(L)}{\vol_{\eucl}(S^{2m-1})}\right)

for all
t
. The arguments in the proof of Theorem
2.21
conclude that
(\mathscr{C},\omega)
is a Riemannian cone. The remaining arguments in this section imply the existence of an automorphism
\Psi\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
such that
\Psi^{*}\omega=\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
, concluding the proof of the Liouville Theorem.
∎

3.
C^{0,\alpha}
-Type Estimate for Kähler Metrics in a Neighborhood of
o

Given the Liouville Theorem for Calabi-Yau cones, we prove a
C^{0,\alpha}
-type estimate for Kähler metrics on
B_{3}(o)
that are uniformly equivalent to a Calabi-Yau cone metric and have scalar curvature controlled in
L^{\infty}(B_{3}(o))
. This requires constructing a novel seminorm analogous to the Hölder-like seminorm defined by Krylov
[
32
, Theorem 3.3.1]
. The seminorm by Krylov is uniformly equivalent to the standard Hölder norm in
\mathbb{R}^{2m}
and computes a weighted distance to a specified class of objects, which for
\mathbb{R}^{2m}
is the set of constants. However, this set does not exist on
\mathscr{C}
unless
\mathscr{C}\cup\{o\}=\mathbb{R}^{2m}
. Furthermore, we want to show that any Kähler metric
\omega
as above is close to
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
around the apex (see Corollary
3.16
). In order to prove this, we need to prove a Hölder estimate for the Laplacian in a neighborhood of the apex
o
. The method of proof is very similar to the estimate for the complex Monge-Ampère equation in Theorem
3.15
and hence also serves as a model for the proof of the nonlinear estimate. The approach is inspired by
[
46
]
and
[
29
]
. In the following sections, all constants may change from line to line.

3.1.
C^{0,\alpha}
-Type Seminorm

The estimates for the Laplacian or complex Monge-Ampère equation rely on comparing the functions or 2-forms to a specified set of comparison objects. We define the comparison set and the Hölder-like seminorm, and then discuss how it relates to the usual
C^{0,\alpha}
-seminorm from Definition
2.5
.

Definition 3.1

(Coordinate Balls)
.

Let
p\in\{r=1\}\subset\mathscr{C}
be a point, and consider a holomorphic coordinate ball
B_{\varrho}(p)
around
p
. Cover
\{r=1\}
with finitely many such balls
B_{\varrho_{i}}(p_{i})
centered at points
p_{i}\in\{r=1\}
for
i=1,\dots,n
. Define the rescaling map
\Phi_{\epsilon}(r,x)=(\epsilon r,x)
. Consider the family

\mathfrak{U}\coloneqq\{B_{\epsilon\varrho_{i}}(\Phi_{\epsilon}(p_{i}))\mid\epsilon>0,\,i=1,\dots,n\},

consisting of all rescalings of the balls
B_{\varrho_{i}}(p_{i})
with coordinates inherited by scaling.
\mathfrak{U}
is then called a family of coordinate balls, and any
U\in\mathfrak{U}
is a coordinate ball of
\mathfrak{U}
.

Definition 3.2

(Comparison set)
.

Let
(\mathscr{C},\omega_{\mathscr{C}})
be a conical Calabi-Yau manifold
\mathscr{C}\cong\mathbb{R}_{+}\times L
. Define the following:

(1)

For
C\geqslant 1
, define

\Sigma_{C}^{2}\coloneqq\{\Psi^{*}(\omega_{\mathscr{C}})\mid\Psi\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C}),\;\frac{1}{C}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant C\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\}

as the set of pullbacks of
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
by automorphisms of
\mathscr{C}
commuting with scaling and bounded uniformly above and below.

(2)

Let
\mathfrak{U}
be a family of coordinate balls. Let
\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
be the collection of
(1,1)
-forms on any coordinate ball
U
that is constant in the associated coordinates. Notice that any element in
\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
is only defined on a coordinate ball and not globally.

(3)

For any element
\pi\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
and set
V\subset{\mathscr{C}}
, define the infinity indicator function

\mathds{1}_{V}(\pi)\coloneqq\begin{cases}1&\text{if $\pi$ is defined on $V$,}\\
\infty&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

More elaborately,
\mathds{1}_{V}(\pi)=1
for
\pi\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
if and only if
V
is contained in the coordinate ball
U
where
\pi
is defined, otherwise
\mathds{1}_{V}(\pi)=\infty
. We assume that
0\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
is globally defined and set
\mathds{1}_{V}(0)=1
for any set
V\subset\mathscr{C}
.

We define the new
C^{0,\alpha}
-type seminorm using the given comparison set
\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
and any other set
\Pi
of globally defined 2-forms on
\mathscr{C}

Definition 3.3

(
{C^{0,\alpha}}^{\prime}
-seminorm)
.

Let
\omega\in\Omega^{2}(U)
be an open set
V\subset\overline{\mathscr{C}}
. Pick a family of coordinate balls
\mathfrak{U}
as in Definition
3.2
. Let
f\colon(0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
be a positive function. Define the Hölder-type
C^{0,\alpha}
-seminorm, namely the
{C^{0,\alpha}}^{\prime}
-seminorm, on
V
by

(3.1)

\displaystyle{}_{\alpha,V,f,\Pi\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varrho,\nu\in(0,\infty)\\
x\in V\end{subarray}}\varrho^{-\alpha}f(\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(o,(B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V)\setminus B_{\nu}(x)))

\displaystyle\times\inf_{(\pi,\eta)\in\Pi\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\mathds{1}_{(B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V)\setminus B_{\nu}(x)}(\eta)\left\lVert\omega-\pi-\eta\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V)\setminus B_{\nu}(x)}.

If
f\equiv 1
, we omit the function in the seminorm and write
[\omega]_{\alpha,V,1,\Pi\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}=[\omega]_{\alpha,V,\Pi\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}
.
The full
{C^{0,\alpha}}^{\prime}
-norm is

\left\lVert\omega\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V,f,\Pi\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}=\left\lVert\omega\right\rVert_{0,V}+[\omega]_{\alpha,V,f,\Pi\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}.

Remark 3.4
.

(1)

We consider
\Pi=\Sigma_{3C}^{2}
in Theorem
3.15
. In the proofs, we also consider
\Pi=\emptyset
or
\Pi=\varrho^{-\alpha}(\Sigma_{3C}^{2}-\pi)
for some
\pi\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}
and
\varrho>0
. In any of these case, the infimum in (
3.1
) is realized and replaced by a minimum.

(2)

On
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and for
\zeta\in\Omega^{k}(V)
, the seminorm

(3.2)

[\zeta]_{\alpha,V,\Sigma_{\text{const}}^{k}}^{\prime}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varrho\in(0,\lambda]\\
x\in V\end{subarray}}\varrho^{-\alpha}\inf_{\pi\in\Sigma_{\text{const}}^{k}}\left\lVert\zeta-\pi\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}

is uniformly equivalent to the usual
C^{0,\alpha}
-seminorm on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
(see

[
32
, Theorem 3.3.1]
) if
\Sigma_{\text{const}}^{k}
is the set of constant
k
-forms on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
.

(3)

All seminorms in Definition
3.3
depend on the family
\mathfrak{U}
of coordinate balls

(4)

The function
f
is an arbitrary weight function that will be used to ensure that the seminorm a priori exists close to the apex.

(5)

The factor
\mathds{1}_{(B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V)\setminus B_{\nu}(x)}(\pi)
ensures that if
\pi\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
is a constant 2-form in a coordinate ball
U
, then
\pi
is actually defined on the set
(B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V)\setminus B_{\nu}(x)
. Recall that
0\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
is globally defined and
\mathds{1}_{(B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V)\setminus B_{\nu}(x)}(0)=1
always.

(6)

In this chapter, all balls, radii, and norms are with respect to
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
.

3.2.
Linear
C^{0,\alpha}
-Estimate

The main proposition of the linear case is the following: A
C^{0,\alpha}
-estimate for functions on
B_{3}(o)
with a sufficiently regular Laplacian.

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
(\mathscr{C},\omega_{\mathscr{C}})
be a conical Calabi-Yau manifold. Let
\omega
be a Kähler metric on
B_{3}(o)
such that

(3.3)

\frac{1}{C}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant\omega\leqslant C\omega_{\mathscr{C}},\quad\left\lVert\Scal(\omega)\right\rVert_{0,B_{3}(o)}\leqslant D.

Assume that
\varphi\colon B_{3}(o)\to\mathbb{R}
is a smooth function satisfying

(3.4)

\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,B_{3}(o)}\leqslant C_{1},\quad\left\lVert\Delta_{\omega}\varphi\right\rVert_{0,B_{3}(o)}\leqslant C_{2},

for
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. Then for all
\alpha\in(0,1)
there exists a constant
C_{3}=C_{3}({\mathscr{C}},\omega_{\mathscr{C}},C,D,C_{1},C_{2},\alpha)
such that

[\varphi]_{\alpha,B_{1}(o)}\leqslant C_{3}.

Remark 3.6
.

In Proposition
3.5
, (
3.3
) can be replaced by the assumption that
\omega=\omega_{C(L)}
is itself a Kähler cone metric, where the manifold
C(L)
does not necessarily support a conical Calabi-Yau metric. The proof is the same, except we no longer choose a sequence
(\omega_{i})
for
\omega
, but instead keep the metric fixed. However, the constant
C_{3}
cannot be chosen uniformly for any choice of Kähler cone metric. Furthermore, the proof now only holds for
\alpha>0
small enough.

We will actually prove Proposition
3.5
via the primed seminorm defined in Definition
3.3
, modified to support functions. For any function
\varphi
on
V
, define

(3.5)

\displaystyle{}_{\alpha,V,f,\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varrho,\nu\in(0,\infty)\\
x\in V\end{subarray}}\varrho^{-\alpha}

\displaystyle f(\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(o,(B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V)\setminus B_{\nu}(x)))

\displaystyle\times\min_{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}}\left\lVert\varphi-\zeta\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V)\setminus B_{\nu}(x)}.

The function
\mathds{1}_{(B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V)\setminus B_{\nu}(x)}
is not necessary as all constant functions are defined globally. The full norm is

\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V,f,\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}=\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,V}+[\varphi]_{\alpha,V,f,\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}.

By the following lemma, this is equivalent to the usual seminorm:

Lemma 3.7
.

On any open
V\subset\mathscr{C}
and
\varphi\in C^{0,\alpha}(V)
, then

\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V}\leqslant 2\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V,\mathbb{C}}^{\prime},

assuming that the right-hand side exists. Conversely, if
\varphi\in C^{0,\alpha}(V)
:

\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V,\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}\leqslant\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V}.

Proof.

Take two points
x,y\in V
and let
\varrho\coloneqq\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(x,y)
. Then

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,V}+\sup_{x\neq y\in V}\frac{|\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)|}{\varrho^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,V}+\sup_{x\neq y\in V}\min_{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}}\varrho^{-\alpha}\left(|\varphi(x)-\zeta+\zeta-\varphi(y)|\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,V}+\sup_{x\neq y\in V}\min_{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}}\varrho^{-\alpha}\left(|\varphi(x)-\zeta|+|\zeta-\varphi(y)|\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,V}+2\sup_{\varrho>0,x\in V}\min_{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}}\varrho^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\varphi-\zeta\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V,\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}.

Conversely:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V,\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,V}+[\varphi]_{\alpha,V,\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,V}+\sup_{\varrho>0,x\in V}\min_{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}}\varrho^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\varphi-\zeta\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,V}+\sup_{\varrho>0,x\in V}\min_{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}}\varrho^{-\alpha}\sup_{y\in B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}|\varphi(y)-\zeta|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V},

where for every
x\in V
we choose
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}
such that
\varphi(x)=\zeta
.
∎

Next, the condition (
3.3
) on the metric in Proposition
3.5
shows that any such sequence
(\omega_{i})
has a subconvergent limit when blowing up.

Lemma 3.8
.

If
(\mathscr{C},\omega_{\mathscr{C}})
is Calabi-Yau and
(\omega_{i})
is a sequence of Kähler metrics on
B_{3}(o)
satisfying the estimates

(3.6)

\frac{1}{C}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant\omega_{i}\leqslant C\omega_{\mathscr{C}},\quad\left\lVert\Scal(\omega_{i})\right\rVert_{0,B_{3}(o)}\leqslant D,

then the blowup
\epsilon_{i}^{-2}\Phi^{*}_{\epsilon_{i}}\omega_{i}
subconverges to
\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
in
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
for all
\epsilon_{i}\to 0
,
\beta<1
, and for some
\Psi\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
.

Proof.

In the proof, we pass to subsequences several times without explicitly mentioning it. Let
\tilde{\omega}_{i}\coloneqq\epsilon_{i}^{-2}\Phi^{*}_{\epsilon_{i}}\omega_{i}
. Writing
\omega_{i}^{m}=e^{F_{i}}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}^{m}
, the scalar curvature condition in (
3.6
) is equivalent to

(3.7)

\left\lVert\Delta_{\omega_{i}}F_{i}\right\rVert_{0,B_{3}(o)}\leqslant D.

Defining the rescaling
\tilde{F}_{i}\coloneqq\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}F_{i}
, then

(3.8)

\left\lVert\Delta_{\tilde{\omega}_{i}}\tilde{F}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,B_{3(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}\leqslant D\epsilon_{i}^{2}.

Proposition
2.8
shows that
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
is uniformly bounded in
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
with uniform bounds for all
i
and all
0<\beta<1
. Hence, as
i\to\infty
, we obtain a
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
-sublimit
\tilde{\omega}_{i}\to\omega_{\infty}
for all
\beta<1
, satisfying the equation

\Scal(\omega_{\infty})=-\Delta_{\omega_{\infty}}\tilde{F}_{\infty}=0

weakly and with
\omega_{\infty}^{m}=e^{\tilde{F}_{\infty}}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}^{m}
. Since
\omega_{\infty}
and
\tilde{F}_{\infty}
are both in
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
, the Schauder estimates
[
20
, Theorem 9.19]
show that
\tilde{F}_{\infty}
is bounded in
C^{2,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
. Using (
2.4
) shows that
\omega_{\infty}
is bounded in
C^{2,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
. Bootstrapping finally implies that
\omega_{\infty}\in C^{\infty}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
. We conclude by the Liouville Theorem (Theorem
2.3
) that
\omega_{\infty}=\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
for some automorphism
\Psi\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
.
∎

3.3.
Proof of Proposition
3.5

3.3.1.
Picking Weight-Function to make Seminorm Well-Defined

As
[\varphi]_{\alpha,B_{1}(o)}
is equivalent to
[\varphi]_{\alpha,B_{1}(o),\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}
by Lemma
3.7
, we give the proof for the
{C^{0,\alpha}}^{\prime}
-seminorm.

A priori, the regular Hölder seminorm
[\varphi]_{\alpha,B_{2}(o)}
does not exist in a neighborhood of
o
. However, we can control the growth of
[\varphi]_{\alpha,B_{2}(o)\setminus B_{\nu}(o)}
as
\nu\to 0
as follows: Take a point
p\in B_{2}(o)
and
\varrho=\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(o,p)
. Apply a rescaling such that
\Phi^{-1}_{\varrho}(p)=\tilde{p}
with
\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(o,\tilde{p})=1
. Define
\tilde{\varphi}\coloneqq\Phi^{*}_{\varrho}\varphi
. Corollary
2.9
provides an
A>0
such that
[\tilde{\varphi}]_{\alpha,B_{\frac{1}{2}}(\tilde{p})}<A
for any
\varphi
satisfying the requirements of Proposition
3.5
. Scaling back, we see:

[\varphi]_{\alpha,B_{\frac{\varrho}{2}}(\tilde{p})}<A\varrho^{-\alpha}.

Therefore:

[\varphi]_{\alpha,B_{\frac{\varrho}{2}}(p)}<A\varrho^{-\alpha},

for any
p\in B_{2}(o)\setminus B_{\varrho}(o)
. The seminorm on
B_{2}(o)\setminus B_{\varrho}(o)
is therefore controlled on radii smaller than
\frac{\varrho}{2}
. For radii greater than this, take
x,y\in B_{2}(o)
with
\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(x,y)\geqslant\frac{\varrho}{2}
. Then

\frac{|\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)|}{\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(x,y)^{\alpha}}\leqslant 2\left(\frac{\varrho}{2}\right)^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\varphi\right\rVert_{0,B_{3}(o)}\leqslant 4\varrho^{-\alpha}C_{1}.

We conclude that, for any
\delta>0
:

[\varphi]_{\alpha,B_{2}(o)\setminus B_{\delta}(o)}\leqslant A\delta^{-\alpha},

and so by Lemma
3.7
:

(3.9)

[\varphi]_{\alpha,B_{2}(o)\setminus B_{\delta}(o),\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}\leqslant A\delta^{-\alpha}.

This leads us to define the following function to control the growth of
[\varphi]_{\alpha,B_{2}(o)\setminus B_{\delta}(o),\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}
as
\delta\to 0
: For any
\delta>0
, define the function
f_{\delta}\colon[0,2]\to[0,1]
by

f_{\delta}(r)=\begin{cases}\delta^{-\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}r^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}&0\leqslant r\leqslant\delta,\\
1&r\in(\delta,1),\\
2-r&r\in[1,2].\end{cases}

The case
r\in[1,2]
is to keep the seminorm away from the outer boundary. Using this seminorm, it is guaranteed that
[\varphi]_{\alpha,B_{2}(o),f_{\delta},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}
always exists for any
\delta>0
. As
\alpha<1
, notice that

\alpha<\frac{1+\alpha}{2}<1.

This will be important later for the application of Proposition
C.1
.

Proceeding by contradiction, assume that there exists a sequence of functions
(\varphi_{i})
and metrics
(\omega_{i})
satisfying the assumptions of the proposition and with

(3.10)

[\varphi_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2}(o),f_{i^{-1}},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}\to\infty

for
\alpha<1
. As
i\to\infty
, the weight function on
B_{1}(o)
becomes identically
1
.

For a sequence
\epsilon_{i}\to 0
, form the rescaled functions
\tilde{\varphi}_{i}\coloneqq\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}\varphi_{i}
and metrics
\tilde{\omega}_{i}\coloneqq\epsilon_{i}^{-2}\Phi^{*}_{\epsilon_{i}}\omega_{i}
, now defined on
B_{3(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)
. The
C^{0}
-norm of
\tilde{\varphi}_{i}
is preserved, but the Hölder seminorm changes:

(3.11)

[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\Phi^{*}_{\epsilon_{i}}f_{i^{-1}},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}=\epsilon_{i}^{\alpha}[\varphi_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2}(o),f_{i^{-1}},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}.

The Laplacian (
3.4
) of
\varphi
scales by

(3.12)

\left\lVert\Delta_{\tilde{\omega}_{i}}\tilde{\varphi}\right\rVert_{0,B_{3(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}\leqslant C_{2}\epsilon_{i}^{2}.

Fix the sequence
(\epsilon_{i})
such that

(3.13)

[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\Phi^{*}_{\epsilon_{i}}f_{i^{-1}},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}=1

for all
i
. The condition
[\varphi_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2}(o),f_{i^{-1}},\mathbb{C}}\to\infty
as
i\to\infty
implies that
\epsilon_{i}\to 0
. Therefore, the metrics
(\tilde{\omega}_{i})
and functions
(\tilde{\varphi}_{i})
are defined on increasingly larger balls as
i\to\infty
, and by Lemma
3.8
, there exists a
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
-sublimit
\omega_{\infty}=\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
for some
\Psi\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
and for all
\beta<1
. Furthermore, by Corollary
2.9
, we also obtain a limiting function
\varphi_{\infty}\in C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
. The next sections utilize this limiting function and metric to obtain a contradiction with (
3.34
).

3.3.2.
Changing Weight-Function and Picking Maximizing Point and Distance.

The previous choice of weight function was picked to ensure that the seminorm (
3.10
) exists for any
i>0
. For the proof, it will be convenient to change the function slightly, and also pick points
x_{i}\in\mathscr{C}
, radii
\varrho_{i},\nu_{i}>0
, and element
\varphi_{i}\in\mathbb{C}
realizing the seminorm. We do this as follows:

For every
i
, pick a point
x_{i}\in\overline{B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}
, radii
\varrho_{i},\nu_{i}>0
, and an element
\zeta_{i}\in\mathbb{C}
realizing at least half of the seminorm
[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}}),\mathbb{C}}
as follows: Define

\delta_{i}\coloneqq\dist_{\omega_{{\mathscr{C}}}}(o,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})),

then

(3.14)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{2}[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}}),\mathbb{C}}\leqslant(\varrho_{i})^{-\alpha}\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta_{i}\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}.

\zeta_{i}
is assumed to be the minimizer on
B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})
. A priori, it could be that
\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})(\delta_{i})\to 0
as
i\to\infty
. For convenience in the proof, we redefine
\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})
to
\tilde{f}_{i}
such that this does not occur. This can happen in two places: if
\delta_{i}<(i\epsilon_{i})^{-1}
, or if
\delta_{i}\in[\epsilon_{i}^{-1},2\epsilon_{i}^{-1}]
:

If
\delta_{i}\geqslant(i\epsilon_{i})^{-1}
already, then define
\tilde{f}_{i}\coloneqq\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})
; otherwise if
\delta_{i}<(i\epsilon_{i})^{-1}
:

\tilde{f}_{i}(r)\coloneqq\begin{cases}(\delta_{i})^{-\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}r^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2}},&0\leqslant r\leqslant\delta_{i},\\
1,&r\in(\delta_{i},\epsilon_{i}^{-1}),\\
2-\epsilon_{i}r,&r\in[\epsilon_{i}^{-1},2\epsilon_{i}^{-1}].\end{cases}

For the weight functions, we have the crucial property:

\frac{\tilde{f}_{i}(r)}{\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})}\leqslant\frac{\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})(r)}{\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})(\delta_{i})}

for any
r\in(0,2\epsilon_{i}^{-1})
. Thus, for any other
x_{i}^{\prime},\varrho_{i}^{\prime},\nu_{i}^{\prime}
with
\delta_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(o,B_{\varrho_{i}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime}))
, then

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta_{i}\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})\frac{\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})}{\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})}\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta_{i}\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})\frac{\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})}{\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})}\frac{\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}f_{i^{-1}}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})}{\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}f_{i^{-1}}(\delta_{i})}(\varrho_{i}^{\prime})^{-\alpha}\min_{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}}\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})(\varrho_{i}^{\prime})^{-\alpha}\min_{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}}\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)},

with the penultimate inequality following from (
3.14
) and
x_{i},\varrho_{i},\nu_{i},\zeta_{i}
realizing at least half of the seminorm in (
3.14
). Thus,
x_{i},\varrho_{i},\nu_{i},\zeta_{i}
also realizes at least half of
[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\tilde{f}_{i},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}
:

\frac{1}{2}[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\tilde{f}_{i},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}\leqslant\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta_{i}\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}.

For the case
\delta_{i}\in[\epsilon_{i}^{-1},2\epsilon_{i}^{-1}]
, then as
\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,B_{3(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}\leqslant C_{1}
and the Laplacian of
\tilde{\varphi}
is bounded by (
3.12
), Corollary
2.9
and Lemma
3.7
imply that
[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)\setminus B_{1}(o),\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}\leqslant\frac{1}{c}
for some constant
c>0
, weight function identically
1
, and on
B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)\setminus B_{1}(o)
. Therefore,

\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\geqslant c,

i.e.
\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})
is bounded from below. This also further shows that
\delta_{i}\leqslant\frac{2-c}{\epsilon_{i}}
. Assuming that
\varrho_{i}
stays bounded as
i\to\infty
, the distance
\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(\partial(B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)),B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}))\to\infty
as
i\to\infty
, i.e. our maximizing ball gets infinitely far away from the outer boundary in the limit.

Finally, as
\tilde{f}_{i}\geqslant\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})
, then
[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\tilde{f}_{i},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}\geqslant[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}}),\mathbb{C}}
. If necessary and without changing notation, make
\epsilon_{i}\to 0
even faster such that

(3.15)

[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\tilde{f}_{i},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}=1

for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
. This rescaling preserves the discussion above, in particular the lower bound
\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\geqslant c
.

3.3.3.
Setting up Contradiction and Obtaining Limiting Function and Metric

We prove Proposition
3.5
by showing the following claim:

Claim:
As
i\to\infty
:

(3.16)

[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\tilde{f}_{i},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}\to 0.

If the claim is true, we obtain a contradiction with (
3.15
) and hence also (
3.10
), proving the theorem.

Proof:
The first step is to show that there exists a
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
-sublimit
\tilde{\varphi}_{i}\to\varphi_{\infty}
for all
\beta<1
. Due to the convergence
\tilde{\omega}_{i}\to\omega_{\infty}
in
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
(see Lemma
3.8
), the bounds coming from (
3.4
), and rescaling:

\left\lVert\Delta_{\tilde{\omega}_{i}}\tilde{\varphi}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,B_{3(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}\leqslant D\epsilon_{i}^{2},

so Corollary
2.9
shows that we obtain a
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
-sublimit
\varphi_{\infty}
. The limit satisfies the equation

(3.17)

\Delta_{\omega_{\infty}}\varphi_{\infty}=0

weakly. Elliptic regularity implies that
\varphi_{\infty}\in C_{\loc}^{\infty}(\mathscr{C})
and satisfies (
3.17
) strongly with
L^{\infty}
-bound coming from (
3.4
), so
\varphi_{\infty}\in\mathbb{C}
by Proposition
C.1
.

We divide the proof of (
3.16
) into three cases:

3.3.4.
Case 1:

\varrho_{i}\to\infty

Since
(\tilde{\varphi}_{i})
is uniformly bounded by assumption and
0\in\mathbb{C}
, we have

\frac{1}{2}[\tilde{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\tilde{f}_{i},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}\leqslant\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\min_{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}}\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta\right\rVert_{B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})}\leqslant C_{1}\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\to 0

as
i\to\infty
, which is as stated in the claim.

3.3.5.
Case 2:

\varrho_{i}
is bounded away from
0
and
\infty
, i.e., there exist constants
b,B>0
such that
b<\varrho_{i}<B
.

Subcase 2.
1)

0<a<\delta_{i}<A<\infty
for some
a,A>0
: The convergence
\tilde{\varphi}_{i}\to\varphi_{\infty}\in\mathbb{C}
in
C^{1,\beta}(K)
for a compact set
K
containing all
B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})
for
i
sufficiently large implies that

\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\min_{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}}\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\cap B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})}\leqslant 2b^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\varphi_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,K}\to 0.

Subcase 2.
2)

\delta_{i}\to 0
: In this case,
B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})
leaves every compact set in
{\mathscr{C}}
, and we cannot use the convergence
\tilde{\varphi}_{i}\to\varphi_{\infty}
in
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
directly. For this part, we assume
\varrho_{i}=1
at all times to ease notation. Note that
\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})=1
, but this does not play a role here.

Since
\tilde{\varphi}_{i}\to\varphi_{\infty}\in\mathbb{C}
in
C_{\loc}^{1,\beta}(\mathscr{C})
, for any small but fixed
\mu>0
and
i
sufficiently large, we have

(3.18)

\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\varphi_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[\mu,2]\times L}\leqslant\mu.

By the Hölder bound (
3.15
) and choosing
x_{i}=o
, there exists
\psi_{i}\in\mathbb{C}
such that

\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2\mu]\times L}\leqslant(2\mu)^{\alpha}.

The triangle inequality now implies that

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\mu,2\mu]\times L}

\displaystyle=\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}-\tilde{\varphi}_{i}+\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\mu,2\mu]\times L}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}-\tilde{\varphi}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\mu,2\mu]\times L}+\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\mu,2\mu]\times L}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mu+(2\mu)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leqslant 3\mu^{\alpha}.

As
\varphi_{\infty},\psi_{i}\in\mathbb{C}
, then

\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\varphi_{\infty}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,(0,2]\times L}\leqslant 3\mu^{\alpha}.

So as
\zeta_{i}\in\mathbb{C}
is the minimizer on
B_{\varrho_{i}}(o)\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(o)\subset[\delta_{i},2]\times L
:

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta_{i}\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\varphi_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2]\times L}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})(\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\varphi_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2\mu]\times L}+\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\varphi_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[2\mu,2]\times L})

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\psi_{i}+\psi_{i}-\varphi_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2\mu]\times L}+\mu

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2\mu]\times L}+\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\psi_{i}-\varphi_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2\mu]\times L}+\mu

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\mu^{\alpha}+3\mu^{\alpha}+\mu,

as stated by the claim as
\mu
can be chosen arbitrarily small for
i
big enough.

Subcase 2.
3)

\delta_{i}\to\infty
: Pick a covering
\mathfrak{U}
of coordinate balls. As
\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(x_{i},o)\to\infty
, and as the coordinate balls
\mathfrak{U}
have increasing size moving away from the apex,
B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})
is always contained in a coordinate ball
U
for
i
sufficiently large. Fix the associated holomorphic coordinates on
U
, and apply translations such that
x_{i}=0
is always the center. We already know that
\dist(x_{i},\partial B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o))\to\infty
, so for every
i
, choose an increasing sequence of balls
B_{r_{i}}(x_{i})\subset B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)
such that
r_{i}\to\infty
and
\frac{r_{i}}{\epsilon_{i}^{-1}}\to 0
. This ensures that
B_{r_{i}}(x_{i})
is always contained in a coordinate ball for
i
large enough.

By the Liouville Theorem (Theorem
2.3
) for cscK metrics and the Liouville Theorem for bounded harmonic functions on
\mathbb{C}^{m}

[
20
, Corollary 3.12]
,
\tilde{\omega}_{i}|_{B_{r_{i}}(x_{i})}\to A^{*}\omega_{\eucl}
in
C_{\loc}^{1,\beta}(\mathbb{C}^{m})
for some
A\in\GL(m,\mathbb{C})
, and
\tilde{\varphi}_{i}|_{B_{r_{i}}(x_{i})}
converges to a constant
\zeta_{\text{const}}\in\mathbb{C}
in
C_{\loc}^{1,\beta}(\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Hence,

\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\min_{\zeta\in\mathbb{C}}\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})}\leqslant b^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\tilde{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta_{\text{const}}\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})}\to 0,\quad i\to\infty,

and the result follows.

3.3.6.
Case 3:

\varrho_{i}\to 0

In this case, we rescale
{\mathscr{C}}
at
o
such that if
\hat{x}_{i}
and
\hat{\varrho}_{i}
are the rescaled coordinates of
x_{i}
and
\varrho_{i}
, respectively, then
\hat{\varrho}_{i}=1
for all
i
. This rescaling decreases the
{C^{0,\alpha}}^{\prime}
-seminorm of
\tilde{\varphi}_{i}
, but after multiplying by
\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}
, the seminorm equals 1 in the rescaled coordinates. The
C^{0}
-norm now blows up, but by subtracting a constant, we can locally control the uniform norm. More precisely, we define:

(1)

\hat{x}_{i}\coloneqq\Phi_{\varrho_{i}}^{-1}(x_{i})
,

(2)

\varrho_{i}^{-2}\Phi_{\varrho_{i}}^{*}(\omega_{\mathscr{C}})=\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
,

(3)

\hat{\omega}_{i}\coloneqq\varrho_{i}^{-2}\Phi_{\varrho_{i}}^{*}\tilde{\omega}_{i}
,

(4)

\hat{\varphi}_{i}\coloneqq\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\Phi_{\varrho_{i}}^{*}\tilde{\varphi}_{i}
,

(5)

\hat{\varrho}_{i}\coloneqq\frac{\varrho_{i}}{\varrho_{i}}=1,\hat{\nu}_{i}\coloneqq\frac{\nu_{i}}{\varrho_{i}}<1,\hat{\delta}_{i}\coloneqq\frac{\delta_{i}}{\varrho_{i}}
,

(6)

\hat{f}_{i}\coloneqq\Phi_{\varrho_{i}}^{*}(\tilde{f}_{i})
.

\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
is invariant under this rescaling as it is a cone metric. As stated above, the maximizing distance is
\hat{\varrho}_{i}=1
and

(3.19)

[\hat{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i}\varrho_{i})^{-1}}(o),\hat{f}_{i},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}=1.

as
\hat{\varphi}_{i}\coloneqq\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\Phi_{\varrho_{i}}^{*}\tilde{\varphi}_{i}
. However, a priori,

\left\lVert\hat{\varphi}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,B_{2(\epsilon_{i}\varrho_{i})^{-1}}(o)}\to\infty

as
i\to\infty
. By construction of
\tilde{f}_{i}
, we have

(3.20)

\hat{f}_{i}(\hat{\delta}_{i})\geqslant c.

Pick a fixed but arbitrary
p\in\{r=1\}
. By defining
\check{\varphi}_{i}\coloneqq\hat{\varphi}_{i}-\hat{\varphi}_{i}(p)
, we ensure that
\check{\varphi}_{i}(p)=0
, and it also satisfies the estimate (
3.19
):

(3.21)

[\check{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i}\varrho_{i})^{-1}}(o),\hat{f}_{i},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}=1.

We again divide Case 3 into three subcases:

Subcase 3.
1)

0<a<\hat{\delta}_{i}<A<\infty
for some
a,A>0
:
We first show that
\check{\varphi}_{i}
is uniformly bounded in
C^{0,\alpha}_{\loc}(B_{2(\epsilon_{i}\varrho_{i})^{-1}}(o))
: Let
\zeta^{R,\nu}\in\mathbb{C}
be the constant realizing the minimum for
[\check{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i}\varrho_{i})^{-1}}(o),\hat{f}_{i},\mathbb{C}}^{\prime}
on
B_{R}(o)\setminus B_{\nu}(o)
. Then (
3.21
) implies that

(3.22)

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{i}(\nu)\left\lVert\check{\varphi}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,B_{R}(o)\setminus B_{\nu}(o)}

\displaystyle\leqslant\hat{f}_{i}(\nu)\left\lVert\check{\varphi}_{i}-\zeta^{R,\nu}\right\rVert_{0,B_{R}(o)\setminus B_{\nu}(o)}+\hat{f}_{i}(\nu)\left\lVert\zeta^{R,\nu}\right\rVert_{0,B_{R}(o)\setminus B_{\nu}(o)}

\displaystyle\leqslant R^{\alpha}+\hat{f}_{i}(\nu)|\zeta^{R,\nu}(p)|

\displaystyle\leqslant R^{\alpha}+\hat{f}_{i}(\nu)|\zeta^{R,\nu}(p)-\check{\varphi}_{i}(p)|+\hat{f}_{i}(\nu)|\check{\varphi}_{i}(p)|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2R^{\alpha}

as
\check{\varphi}_{i}(p)=0
by construction. Notice that
\left\lVert\zeta^{R,\nu}\right\rVert_{0,B_{R}(o)\setminus B_{\nu}(o)}=|\zeta^{R,\nu}(p)|
as
\zeta^{R,\nu}
is a constant. As
\hat{f}_{i}(\hat{\delta}_{i})=1
and
\hat{\delta}_{i}<A<\infty
for some
A>0
, the decay condition of
\hat{f}_{i}
and (
3.22
) show that

(3.23)

|\check{\varphi}_{i}|\leqslant C\begin{cases}r^{-\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}&r\leqslant 1,\\
r^{\alpha}&r>1.\end{cases}

Due to the
C^{0}_{\loc}(B_{2(\epsilon_{i}\varrho_{i})^{-1}}(o))
-bound (
3.23
) of
\check{\varphi}_{i}
,
\epsilon_{i}\to 0
, and

\left\lVert\Delta_{\hat{\omega}_{i}}\check{\varphi}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,B_{3(\epsilon_{i}\varrho_{i})^{-1}}(o)}\leqslant C_{2}\epsilon_{i}^{2}\varrho_{i}^{2-\alpha},

we obtain a
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
-limit
\check{\varphi}_{\infty}
and a
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
-limit
\hat{\omega}_{\infty}=\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
for all
\beta<1
satisfying

(3.24)

\Delta_{\hat{\omega}_{\infty}}\check{\varphi}_{\infty}=0

weakly, and hence strongly by elliptic regularity.
\check{\varphi}_{\infty}
also has the growth rate (
3.23
) as
\hat{\delta}_{i}<A
, so by Proposition
C.1
, Ricci-flatness of
\hat{\omega}_{\infty}
, and
\alpha<\frac{1+\alpha}{2}<1
, we conclude that
\check{\varphi}_{\infty}\in\mathbb{C}
. As
\hat{\varrho}_{i}=1
, then

1=[\check{\varphi}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i}\varrho_{i})^{-1}}(o),\hat{f}_{i},\mathbb{C}}\leqslant 2\left\lVert\check{\varphi}_{i}-\check{\varphi}_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,K}\to 0,\quad i\to\infty,

for some compact set
K
containing all
B_{1}(\hat{x}_{i})\setminus B_{\hat{\nu}_{i}}(\hat{x}_{i})
, showing the claim in this case.

Subcase 3.
2)

\hat{\delta}_{i}=\dist_{\omega_{{\mathscr{C}}}}(B_{1}(\hat{x}_{i})\setminus B_{\hat{\nu}_{i}}(\hat{x}_{i}),o)\to 0
: Subcase 3.1 showed that
\check{\varphi}_{i}\to\check{\varphi}_{\infty}
in
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
. Given this, the proof is exactly as in subcase 2.2.

Subcase 3.
3)

\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(\hat{x}_{i},o)\to\infty
: As in subcase 2.2, assume that
B_{1}(\hat{x}_{i})
is always contained in a coordinate ball for
i
great enough, and apply a translation in the associated holomorphic coordinates such that
\hat{x}_{i}=0
. Fix a ball
B_{r_{i}}(\hat{x}_{i})
for any
i
such that
r_{i}\to\infty
and
B_{r_{i}}(\hat{x}_{i})
is always contained in a coordinate ball.

Redefine
\check{\varphi}_{i}\coloneqq\hat{\varphi}_{i}-\hat{\varphi}_{i}(\hat{x}_{i})
, then the estimates of Subcase 3.1 show that

\left|\check{\varphi}_{i}|_{B_{r_{i}}(\hat{x}_{i})}\right|\leqslant Cr^{\alpha}

for
0\leqslant r\leqslant r_{i}
. The estimate of the Laplacian also holds:

\left\lVert\Delta_{\hat{\omega}_{i}}\check{\varphi}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,B_{3(\epsilon_{i}\varrho_{i})^{-1}}(o)}\leqslant C_{2}\epsilon_{i}^{2}\varrho_{i}^{2-\alpha}.

Therefore, we obtain
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathbb{C}^{m})
-sublimits
\check{\varphi}_{\infty},\hat{\omega}_{\infty}
for
\beta<1
satisfying
\check{\varphi}_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(r^{\alpha})
and
\Delta_{\hat{\omega}_{\infty}}\check{\varphi}_{\infty}=0
weakly. By the Liouville Theorem (Theorem
2.3
), then
\hat{\omega}_{\infty}=A^{*}\omega_{\eucl}
for some matrix
A\in\GL(m,\mathbb{C})
, and hence the usual Liouville Theorem for harmonic functions with sublinear growth (or see Proposition
C.1
) implies that
\check{\varphi}_{\infty}\in\mathbb{C}
. This again contradicts
\hat{\varrho}_{i}=1
and (
3.21
) by the arguments in subcase 2.3.

3.4.
Nonlinear
C^{0,\alpha}
-Estimate: Preliminaries

After proving the linear Schauder estimate in Proposition
3.5
, we are now in a position to prove an analogous Evans-Krylov estimate for the complex Monge-Ampère equation on
(\mathscr{C},\omega_{\mathscr{C}})
. The method of proof is very similar to Proposition
3.5
, except that there no longer is a concept of globally constant 2-forms as there is for functions. Therefore, we include
\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
in the comparison set to handle the case
x_{i}\to\infty
and get control over the regular Hölder seminorm. This section provides some necessary results for the regularity of the comparison sets
\Sigma_{3C}^{2}
and
\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
.

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
K^{\prime}\Subset K^{^{\circ}}
,
K\Subset\mathscr{C}
be compact sets, and fix
\beta\in(0,1)
. Take a Kähler form
\omega
on
K
with
\frac{1}{C}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant\omega\leqslant C\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
. Pick any
\Psi\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
such that
\frac{1}{3C}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant 3C\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
.
Assume that

\left\lVert\Scal\omega\right\rVert_{0,K}\leqslant\delta,\quad\left\lVert\omega-\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\right\rVert_{0,K}\leqslant\delta

for
\delta\leqslant 1
. Then

\left\lVert\omega-\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\right\rVert_{0,\beta,K^{\prime}}\leqslant B\delta,

for any
\beta<1
and
B=B(C,K,K^{\prime},\beta)
independent of
\Psi
and
\delta>0
.

Proof.

Choose a sequence of compact sets

K^{\prime}\Subset(K^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\circ}\subset K^{\prime\prime\prime}\Subset(K^{\prime\prime})^{\circ}\subset K^{\prime\prime}\subset K^{\circ}.

By proposition
2.8
, then
\left\lVert\omega\right\rVert_{1,\beta,K^{\prime\prime}}\leqslant A
for some constant
A>0
and any
\beta<1
as
\delta\leqslant 1
. Similarly,
\left\lVert\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\right\rVert_{1,\beta,K^{\prime\prime}}\leqslant A
as
\frac{1}{3C}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant 3C\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
. Write
e^{G}\omega^{m}=(\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}})^{m}
. The condition
\left\lVert\Scal\omega\right\rVert_{0,K}\leqslant\delta
is equivalent to
\left\lVert\Delta_{\omega}G\right\rVert_{0,K}\leqslant\delta
. Then:

\displaystyle e^{G}\omega^{m}

\displaystyle=(\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}})^{m}

\displaystyle=(\omega+(\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}-\omega))^{m}

\displaystyle=\omega^{m}+m\omega^{m-1}\wedge(\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}-\omega)+\mathcal{O}((\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}-\omega)^{2}).

For every point
p\in K^{\prime\prime\prime}
and by
[
11
, Lemma 2.1]
, there is a ball
B_{\epsilon}(p)\subset K^{\prime\prime}
with a
C^{2}
-potential
f
such that
i\partial\overline{\partial}f=\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}-\omega
and
C^{0}(B_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(p))
-norm uniformly bounded by
\delta
. Then:

(3.25)

m\Delta_{\omega}f=m\tr_{\omega}(\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}-\omega)=(e^{G}-1)+\mathcal{O}((\omega-\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}})^{2}).

Cover
K^{\prime\prime\prime}
with finitely many such balls. Since:

\left\lVert\Delta_{\omega}G\right\rVert_{0,K^{\prime\prime}}\leqslant C\delta,\qquad\left\lVert e^{G}-1\right\rVert_{0,K}<C^{\prime}\left\lVert\omega-\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\right\rVert_{0,K}\leqslant C^{\prime}\delta,

then
\left\lVert G\right\rVert_{0,K}\leqslant C^{\prime}\delta
, so Corollary
2.9
applied to each
B_{\epsilon}(p)
implies that:

\displaystyle\left\lVert G\right\rVert_{1,\beta,K^{\prime\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C^{\prime}\left(\left\lVert G\right\rVert_{0,K^{\prime\prime}}+\left\lVert\Delta_{\omega}G\right\rVert_{0,K^{\prime\prime}}\right)\leqslant C^{\prime}\delta,

for all
\beta<1
and
C^{\prime}
depending on
\beta
. Using the product rule, this now lifts to
e^{G}-1
:

\left\lVert e^{G}-1\right\rVert_{1,\beta,K^{\prime\prime\prime}}\leqslant C^{\prime}\delta.

We estimate the remaining terms on the right-hand side as:

\displaystyle\left\lVert({\omega}-\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}})^{l}\right\rVert_{0,\beta,K^{\prime\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\leqslant D^{\prime}\left\lVert{\omega}-\Psi^{*}{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}\right\rVert_{0,K^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{l-1}\left\lVert{\omega}-{\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}\right\rVert_{0,\beta,K^{\prime\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\leqslant D^{\prime}\left\lVert{\omega}-\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\right\rVert_{0,K^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\leqslant D^{\prime}\delta

for any
l\geqslant 2
. The result follows by applying the Schauder estimates for
\Delta_{{\omega}}
to (
3.25
).
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

The set
\{\Psi\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})\mid\frac{1}{C}\leqslant\left\lVert\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\right\rVert_{0,K}\leqslant C\}
is compact for any constant
C\geqslant 1
and compact set
K\in\mathscr{C}
such that the interior
K^{^{\circ}}
contains a level set
\{r=c\}\subset K^{^{\circ}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.27
, there is an embedding
\mathscr{C}\to\mathbb{C}^{N}
such that the coordinate functions
z_{1},\dots,z_{N}
are linearly independent holomorphic and homogeneous functions on
\mathscr{C}
and
\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})\hookrightarrow\GL(N,\mathbb{C})
. Hence, we identify
\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
with a closed subgroup of
\GL(N,\mathbb{C})
. Then:

\displaystyle C\geqslant\left\lVert\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\right\rVert_{0,K}

\displaystyle=\max_{p\in K}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in T_{p}\mathscr{C}\\
\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}=1\end{subarray}}|g_{\mathscr{C}}(D\Psi(v),D\Psi(v))|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{C^{2}}\max_{p\in K}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in T_{p}\mathscr{C}\\
\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{\omega_{\eucl}}=1\end{subarray}}|g_{\eucl}(\Psi v,\Psi v)|^{\frac{1}{2}},

where we have identified
T_{p}\mathscr{C}\subset\mathbb{C}^{N}
and
\Psi\in\GL(N,\mathbb{C})
by the embedding
\mathscr{C}\to\mathbb{C}^{N}
above. Next, we show that

\Span_{\mathbb{C}}\left\{\bigcup_{p\in K}T_{p}\mathscr{C}\right\}=\mathbb{C}^{N}.

Assume not, then there exist constants
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{N}
such that

(3.26)

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}z_{i}(p)=\text{constant},\quad\text{for all $p\in K$}.

By homogeneity, and since
K^{^{\circ}}
contains a level set
\{r=c\}
, it follows that (
3.26
) splits into sums of coordinates with the same homogeneity, all equal to zero. By homogeneity, then

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}z_{i}(p)=0,\quad\text{for all $p\in\mathscr{C}$},

contradicting the fact that
z_{1},\dots,z_{N}
are linearly independent on
\mathscr{C}
. The desired result follows, and we conclude that

\displaystyle C\geqslant\left\lVert\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\right\rVert_{0,K}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{C^{2}}\max_{p\in K}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in T_{p}\mathscr{C}\\
\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{\omega_{\eucl}}=1\end{subarray}}|g_{\eucl}(\Psi v,\Psi v)|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=D\max_{v\in S^{2N-1}\subset\mathbb{C}^{N}}|g_{\eucl}(\Psi v,\Psi v)|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=D\left\lVert\Psi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\text{Op}}(K)},

where
\left\lVert\Psi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\text{Op}}(K)}
is the operator norm. The constant
\frac{1}{C^{2}}
was changed as
\bigcup_{p\in K}T_{p}\mathscr{C}
might not equal
\mathbb{C}^{N}
, only the span. By linear combinations, we can therefore estimate

\max_{v\in S^{2N-1}\subset\mathbb{C}^{N}}|g_{\eucl}(\Psi v,\Psi v)|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

GL(N,\mathbb{C})
lies in the finite-dimensional vector space of
N\times N
matrices, and the operator norms extend to this space. All norms are equivalent on a finite-dimensional vector space, so if
\Psi=(g_{ij})
:

|g_{ij}|\leqslant D^{\prime}.

Compactness now follows by
\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
being a closed subgroup of
\GL(N,\mathbb{C})
under the embedding induced by
\mathscr{C}\to\mathbb{C}^{N}
.
∎

Lemma 3.11
.

If a sequence
\Phi_{i}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
with
\frac{1}{C}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant\Phi_{i}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant C\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
converges to an element
\Psi_{\infty}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
in
C^{\infty}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
, then there exists a subsequence
(i_{k})
and an isometry
F\in\Isom(\omega_{\mathscr{C}})\cap\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
such that
\Phi_{i_{k}}\circ F\to\Psi_{\infty}
in
C^{\infty}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
as automorphisms.

Proof.

Assume that
\Phi_{i}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\to\Psi_{\infty}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
in
C^{\infty}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
. By Lemma
3.10
, the set
\{\Phi_{i}\}
is compact and subconverges to an element
\Phi_{\infty}\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
such that
\Phi_{\infty}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}=\Psi_{\infty}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
. Define
F\coloneqq\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\circ\Psi
, an isometry by definition, and the result follows.
∎

Corollary 3.12
.

Assume that
\Phi_{i}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\to\Phi_{\infty}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
for
\Phi_{i},\Phi_{\infty}\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
with
\frac{1}{C}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant\Phi_{i}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant C\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
and convergence in
C^{\infty}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
. For any closed
(1,1)
-form
\eta\in\Omega^{2}(\mathscr{C})
such that
\tr_{\Phi_{\infty}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}\eta=\text{constant}
and
\mathscr{L}_{r\partial_{r}}\eta=2\eta
, by taking a subsequence of
\Phi_{i}
we can find a sequence
\Psi_{i}\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
such that

\frac{\Psi_{i}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}-\Phi_{i}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}{i^{-1}}-\eta\to 0,\quad i\to\infty.

in
C^{\infty}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
.

Proof.

By applying an automorphism, we assume without loss of generality that
\Phi_{\infty}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}=\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
. By Proposition
C.5
, write
\eta=i\partial\overline{\partial}(u+cr^{2})
for some
c\in\mathbb{R}
and a 2-homogeneous,
\xi
-invariant function
u
harmonic with respect to
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
. First assume that
c=0
so that
\eta
is trace-free. By Theorem
2.26
,
\nabla u
is a holomorphic vector field; hence its flow generates a 1-parameter subgroup
(\Psi_{t})\subset\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
. By definition:

\omega_{\mathscr{C}}(\nabla u,\cdot)=g_{\mathscr{C}}(\nabla u,J\cdot)=du(J\cdot)=d^{c}u,

so

(3.27)

\frac{d}{dt}\Bigg|_{t=0}\left(\frac{\Psi_{t}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}-\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}{t}\right)=\mathscr{L}_{\nabla u}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}=d(\omega_{\mathscr{C}}(\nabla u,\cdot))=dd^{c}u=\eta.

This proves the corollary if
\Phi_{i}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}=\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
is fixed. If not, then by taking a subsequence and precomposing with a fixed
F\in\Aut_{\Scl}^{*}(\omega_{\mathscr{C}})
, we assume that
\Phi_{i}\to\Id\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
in
C^{\infty}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
by Lemma
3.11
. Therefore:

(\Phi_{i}^{*}-\Id^{*})\left(\frac{\Psi_{\frac{1}{i}}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}-\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}{\frac{1}{i}}\right)\to 0,\quad i\to\infty,

due to the convergence
\Phi_{t}\to\Id
. We conclude that
\tilde{\Psi}_{i}\coloneqq\Psi_{\frac{1}{i}}\circ\Phi_{i}
is the desired sequence for the corollary if
c=0
. If
c\neq 0
, multiply
\tilde{\Psi}_{i}
by
(1+\frac{c}{i})
such that (
3.27
) produces an extra
c\omega_{\mathscr{C}}=c\,i\partial\overline{\partial}r^{2}
as
i\to\infty
.
∎

We also need the following regularity result on
\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
:

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
\eta\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
be defined on a coordinate ball
B_{\varrho}(x)\in\mathfrak{U}
. Pick
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Denote
\nu
as
\nu=\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(o,x)>0
. Then
\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V}\leqslant C^{\prime}(1+\nu^{-\alpha})|\eta(y)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}
for any
y\in B_{\varrho}(x)
and for some
C^{\prime}>0
independent of
B_{\varrho}(x)
,
y
, and
\eta
.

Proof.

Recall that the coordinate ball
B_{\varrho}(x)\in\mathfrak{U}
and
\eta\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
were defined by scaling elements and a ball centered at a point
p\in\{r=1\}
. Rescale by
\nu^{-1}
such that
\Phi_{\nu}^{-1}(x)=p
,
\tilde{\varrho}=\frac{\varrho}{\nu}
, and
\tilde{\eta}=\nu^{-2}\Phi_{\nu}^{*}(\eta)
. As there are only finitely many coordinate balls centered around
\{r=1\}
, there exists a uniform constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

\left\lVert\tilde{\eta}\right\rVert_{0,B_{\tilde{\varrho}}(p)}\leqslant C^{\prime}|\tilde{\eta}(y)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}

for any
y\in B_{\tilde{\varrho}}(p)
. As the set of constant
(1,1)
-forms in the associated coordinates of
B_{\tilde{\varrho}}(p)
is finite-dimensional and all elements are smooth, there exists a constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
such that

\left\lVert\tilde{\eta}\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,B_{\tilde{\varrho}}(p)}\leqslant C^{\prime\prime}\left\lVert\tilde{\eta}\right\rVert_{0,B_{\tilde{\varrho}}(p)}\leqslant C^{\prime}|\tilde{\eta}(y)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}.

The lemma now follows by scaling back.
∎

We finally show that the regular Hölder norm controls the primed norm:

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
V\subset\mathscr{C}
be open with
\nu\coloneqq\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(o,V)>0
. Then for any form
\psi\in\Omega^{1,1}(V)
:

\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V,\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}\leqslant\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V}+\nu^{-\alpha}B\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,V},

with
B>0
independent of
V
.

Proof.

Take a point
x\in V
and
\varrho>0
. Then

\displaystyle\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V,\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,V}+[\psi]_{\alpha,V,\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,V}+\sup_{\varrho>0,x\in V}\min_{\eta\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\varrho^{-\alpha}\mathds{1}_{V\cap B_{\varrho}(x)}(\eta)\left\lVert\psi-\eta\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,V}+\sup_{\varrho>0,x\in V}\min_{\eta\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\varrho^{-\alpha}\mathds{1}_{V\cap B_{\varrho}(x)}(\eta)\sup_{y\in B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}|\psi(y)-\eta(y)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,V}+\sup_{\varrho>0,x\in V}\min_{\eta\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\varrho^{-\alpha}\mathds{1}_{V\cap B_{\varrho}(x)}(\eta)\sup_{y\in B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}\Big(|\textbf{P}_{xy}\psi(y)-\psi(x)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+|\psi(x)-\eta(x)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}+|\eta(x)-\textbf{P}_{xy}\eta(y)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}\Big).

The term

\sup_{\varrho>0,x\in V}\varrho^{-\alpha}\sup_{y\in B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}|\textbf{P}_{xy}\psi(y)-\psi(x)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}=[\psi]_{\alpha,V}

equals the regular Hölder seminorm.

To estimate the other terms, fix a choice of
x
and
\varrho
. Consider the first of two possibilities: If
B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V
is contained in a coordinate ball, then choose some
\eta\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
defined in a coordinate ball
U
containing
B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V
and such that
\eta(x)=\psi(x)
. Continuing:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,V}+\min_{\eta\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\varrho^{-\alpha}\mathds{1}_{V\cap B_{\varrho}(x)}(\eta)\sup_{y\in B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}\Big(|\textbf{P}_{xy}\psi(y)-\psi(x)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+|\psi(x)-\eta(x)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}+|\eta(x)-\textbf{P}_{xy}\eta(y)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V}+\varrho^{-\alpha}\sup_{y\in B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}\Big(|\eta(x)-\textbf{P}_{xy}\eta(y)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}\Big).

By Lemma
3.13
and
\eta=\psi(x)\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
, it follows that

\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,B_{\varrho}(x)}\leqslant C^{\prime}(1+\nu^{-\alpha})|\psi(x)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}\leqslant C^{\prime}(1+\nu^{-\alpha})\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,V},

so we conclude:

\varrho^{-\alpha}\sup_{y\in B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}\Big(|\eta(x)-\textbf{P}_{xy}\eta(y)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}\Big)\leqslant C^{\prime}\nu^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,V}.

This proves the lemma when
B_{\varrho}(x)
is contained in a coordinate ball.

Now assume that
B_{\varrho}(x)
is not contained in a coordinate ball. Then
\eta=0
is the only possibility. By the scaling behavior of the coordinate balls, it follows that there exists a constant
b>0
such that
b\nu\leqslant\varrho
, otherwise
B_{\varrho}(x)
would be contained in a coordinate ball. Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,V}+\min_{\eta\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\varrho^{-\alpha}\mathds{1}_{V\cap B_{\varrho}(x)}(\eta)\sup_{y\in B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}\Big(|\textbf{P}_{xy}\psi(y)-\psi(x)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+|\psi(x)-\eta(x)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}+|\eta(x)-\textbf{P}_{xy}\eta(y)|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V}+(1+\nu^{-\alpha})b^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho}(x)\cap V}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,V}+(1+\nu^{-\alpha})b^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert_{0,V},

finishing the proof of the lemma.
∎

3.5.
Nonlinear
C^{0,\alpha}
-Estimate and Proof

3.5.1.
Statement of Main Theorem

Theorem 3.15
.

Let
(\mathscr{C},\omega_{\mathscr{C}})
be a Calabi-Yau cone with cone metric
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
, and let
\omega
be a Kähler metric on
B_{3}(o)\subset\mathscr{C}
such that

(3.28)

\frac{1}{C}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant\omega\leqslant C\omega_{\mathscr{C}},\quad\left\lVert\Scal(\omega)\right\rVert_{0,B_{3}(o)}\leqslant D,

for some constants
C,D>0
. Then for any
\alpha=\alpha(\mathscr{C},\omega_{\mathscr{C}})>0
small enough, there exists a constant
C^{\prime}
with

[\omega]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{1}(o),\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\leqslant C^{\prime},

where
\alpha=\alpha(\mathscr{C},\omega_{\mathscr{C}})
and
C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}(\mathscr{C},C,D,\alpha,\omega_{\mathscr{C}},\mathfrak{U})
are independent of
\omega
.

3.5.2.
Proof: Picking Weight-Function to make Seminorm Well-Defined

The proof proceeds by contradiction and is similar to Proposition
3.5
. All constants might change from line to line below. First, for any
\omega
satisfying (
3.28
), the seminorm
[\omega]_{\alpha,B_{1}(o),\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}
might not exist a priori. However, as in Proposition
3.5
, we can control the growth of
[\omega]_{\alpha,B_{1}(o)\setminus B_{\delta}(o),\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}
as
\delta\to 0
as follows: Take a point
p\in B_{2}(o)
with
\varrho=\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(o,p)
. Apply a rescaling such that
\Phi^{-1}_{\varrho}(p)=\tilde{p}
with
\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(o,\tilde{p})=1
. Define
\tilde{\omega}\coloneqq\varrho^{-2}\Phi^{*}_{\varrho}(\omega)
. The regularity of the complex Monge-Ampère equation provides an
A>0
such that
[\tilde{\omega}]_{\alpha,B_{\frac{1}{2}}(\tilde{p})}<A
for any
\omega
satisfying the requirements of Theorem
3.15
. Rescaling back, we conclude as in Proposition
3.5
:

[\omega]_{\alpha,B_{1}(o)\setminus B_{\delta}(o)}<A\delta^{-\alpha}.

Lemma
3.14
transforms this into an estimate on the primed seminorm:

(3.29)

[\omega]_{\alpha,B_{1}(o)\setminus B_{\delta}(o),\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}\leqslant A\delta^{-\alpha}.

For any fixed
\Psi\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
and as
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
is 2-homogeneous, then by Lemma
3.14
:

[\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}]_{\alpha,B_{1}(o)\setminus B_{\delta}(o),\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}<A\delta^{-\alpha}.

Hence, the seminorm (
3.29
) will not blow up any faster at the apex by including
\Sigma_{3C}^{2}
. We conclude that:

(3.30)

[\omega]_{\alpha,B_{1}(o)\setminus B_{\delta}(o),\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}\leqslant A\delta^{-\alpha}.

This leads us to define the following function to control the growth of
[\omega]_{\alpha,B_{1}(o),\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}
at
o
: For any
\delta>0
, define the function
f_{\delta}\colon[0,2]\to[0,1]
by

f_{\delta}(r)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\delta^{-\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}r^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}&0\leqslant r\leqslant\delta,\\
1&r\in(\delta,1),\\
2-r&r\in[1,2].\end{cases}

Using this seminorm, it is guaranteed that
[\omega]_{\alpha,B_{2}(o),f_{\delta},\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}
always exists for any
\delta>0
.

Now, assume that there is a sequence of Kähler metrics
(\omega_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
on
B_{3}(o)
such that the following holds:

(1)

\frac{1}{C}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\leqslant\omega_{i}\leqslant C\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
,

(2)

\left\lVert\Scal(\omega_{i})\right\rVert_{0,B_{3}(o)}\leqslant D
,

(3)

[\omega_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2}(o),f_{i^{-1}},\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\to\infty
as
i\to\infty
.

If no such sequence exists, then the theorem follows because
f_{i^{-1}}\to 1
as
i\to\infty
in
C_{\loc}^{0}(B_{1}(o))
. In deriving a contradiction, we pass to a subsequence several times without explicitly mentioning it.

For a sequence
(\epsilon_{i})
and
\omega_{i}=e^{F_{i}}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}^{m}
, form the rescaled metric
\tilde{\omega}_{i}\coloneqq\Phi^{*}_{\epsilon_{i}}(\epsilon_{i}^{-2}\omega_{i})
and
\tilde{F}_{i}\coloneqq\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}F_{i}
as in Proposition
3.5
, now a metric defined on
B_{3(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)
. This rescaling preserves the
C^{0}
-norm of
\omega_{i}
by scale invariance of
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
, but the
{C^{0,\alpha}}^{\prime}
-seminorm of
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
shrinks:

(3.31)

\displaystyle{}^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}}),\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}=\epsilon_{i}^{\alpha}[\omega_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2}(o),f_{i^{-1}},\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}.

Fix the rescaled seminorm along the blowup by choosing
(\epsilon_{i})
such that

(3.32)

\displaystyle{}^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}}),\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}=1

for every
i
. The condition
[\omega_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2}(o),f_{i^{-1}},\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\to\infty
as
i\to\infty
implies that
\epsilon_{i}\to 0
. Therefore, the metrics in the sequence
(\tilde{\omega}_{i})
are defined on increasingly larger balls as
i\to\infty
, and by Lemma
3.8
there exists a
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}
-sublimit
\omega_{\infty}=\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
for some
\Psi\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
and for all
\beta<1
. The next sections utilize this limiting metric to obtain a contradiction with (
3.32
).

3.5.3.
Changing Weight-Function and Picking Maximizing Point and Distance.

The previous choice of weight function was picked to ensure that the seminorm
[\omega_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2}(o),f_{i^{-1}},\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}
exists for any
i>0
. For the proof, it will be convenient to change the function slightly, and also pick points
x_{i}\in\mathscr{C}
, radii
\varrho_{i},\nu_{i}>0
, and elements
\pi_{i}\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2},\eta_{i}\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
realizing the seminorm. We do this as follows:

For every
i
, pick a point
x_{i}\in\overline{2B_{\epsilon_{i}^{-1}}(o)}
and radii
\varrho_{i},\nu_{i}>0
realizing at least half of the seminorm
[\tilde{\omega}_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}}),\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}
. Define
\delta_{i}\coloneqq\dist_{\omega_{{\mathscr{C}}}}(o,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}))
, then this means:

(3.33)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{2}[\tilde{\omega}_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}}),\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\\
\leqslant&\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})(\delta_{i})\min_{(\pi,\eta)\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\mathds{1}_{B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})}(\eta)\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\pi-\eta\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}.\end{aligned}

A priori, it could be that
\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})(\delta_{i})\to 0
as
i\to\infty
. For convenience in the proof and as in Proposition
3.5
, we redefine
\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})
to
\tilde{f}_{i}
such that this does not occur. This can happen in two places: if
\delta_{i}<(i\epsilon_{i})^{-1}
, or if
\delta_{i}\in[\epsilon_{i}^{-2},2\epsilon_{i}^{-1}]
:

If
\delta_{i}\geqslant(i\epsilon_{i})^{-1}
already, then define
\tilde{f}_{i}\coloneqq\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})
; otherwise if
\delta_{i}<(i\epsilon_{i})^{-1}
:

\tilde{f}_{i}(r)\coloneqq\begin{cases}(\delta_{i})^{-\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}r^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2}},&0\leqslant r\leqslant\delta_{i},\\
1,&r\in(\delta_{i},\epsilon_{i}^{-1}),\\
2-\epsilon_{i}r,&r\in[\epsilon_{i}^{-1},2\epsilon_{i}^{-1}].\end{cases}

For the weight functions, we have the crucial property:

\frac{\tilde{f}_{i}(r)}{\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})}\leqslant\frac{\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})(r)}{\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})(\delta_{i})}

for any
r\in(0,2\epsilon_{i}^{-1})
. Let
(\pi_{i},\eta_{i})\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
be the minimizers of (
3.32
) on
B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})
. Thus, for any other
x_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C},\varrho_{i}^{\prime},\nu_{i}^{\prime}>0
with
\delta_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(o,B_{\varrho_{i}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}^{\prime}))
, then

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\pi_{i}-\eta_{i}\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})\frac{\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})}{\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})}\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\pi_{i}-\eta_{i}\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})\frac{\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})}{\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})}\frac{\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}f_{i^{-1}}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})}{\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}f_{i^{-1}}(\delta_{i})}(\varrho_{i}^{\prime})^{-\alpha}\min_{(\pi,\eta)\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\pi-\eta\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i}^{\prime})(\varrho_{i}^{\prime})^{-\alpha}\min_{(\pi,\eta)\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\pi-\eta\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)},

with the penultimate inequality following from (
3.33
) and
x_{i},\varrho_{i},\nu_{i},\pi_{i},\eta_{i}
realizing at least half of the seminorm. Thus,
x_{i},\varrho_{i},\nu_{i},\pi_{i},\eta_{i}
also realizes at least half of
[\tilde{\omega}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\tilde{f}_{i},\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}
:

\frac{1}{2}[\tilde{\omega}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\tilde{f}_{i},\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}\leqslant\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\pi_{i}-\eta_{i}\right\rVert_{0,(B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}))\cap B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}.

For the case
\delta_{i}\in[\epsilon_{i}^{-1},2\epsilon_{i}^{-1}]
, then as
\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,B_{3(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)}\leqslant C_{1}
and the scalar curvatures scales by:

\left\lVert\Scal(\tilde{\omega}_{i})\right\rVert_{0,B_{3(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}(o)}}\leqslant D\epsilon_{i}^{2},

regularity of the complex Monge-Ampère equation and Lemma
3.14
implies that

\displaystyle[\tilde{\omega}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)\setminus B_{1}(o),1,\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)\setminus B_{1}(o),1,\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)\setminus B_{1}(o),1,\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)\setminus B_{1}(o),1,\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}+\left\lVert\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)\setminus B_{1}(o),1,\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)\setminus B_{1}(o)}+\left\lVert\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\right\rVert_{0,\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)\setminus B_{1}(o)}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{c}

for some constant
c>0
and weight function identically
1
. Therefore,

\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\geqslant c,

i.e.
\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})
is bounded from below. This also further shows that
\delta_{i}\leqslant\frac{2-c}{\epsilon_{i}}
. So if
\varrho_{i}
stays bounded, the distance
\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(\partial(B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)),B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i}))\to\infty
as
i\to\infty
, i.e. our maximizing ball becomes infinitely far away from the outer boundary in the limit.

Finally, as
\tilde{f}_{i}\geqslant\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}})
, then

[\tilde{\omega}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\tilde{f}_{i},\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}\geqslant[\tilde{\omega}_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\Phi_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}(f_{i^{-1}}),\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}=1.

If necessary and without changing notation, make
\epsilon_{i}\to 0
even faster such that

(3.34)

[\tilde{\omega}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\tilde{f}_{i},\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}=1

for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
. This rescaling preserves the discussion above, in particular the lower bound
\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\geqslant c
.

3.5.4.
Setting up Contradiction and Obtaining Limiting Function and Metric

Due to Lemma
3.8
, there exists a sublimit
\tilde{\omega}_{i}\to\omega_{\infty}=\Psi^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
in
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
for all
\beta<1
and for some
\Psi\in\Aut_{\Scl}(\mathscr{C})
. Using this, we prove the following claim:

Claim:
The sequence
(\tilde{\omega}_{i})
satisfies

[\tilde{\omega}_{i}]_{\alpha,B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o),\tilde{f}_{i},\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}\to 0,\quad i\to\infty.

This is obviously a contradiction with (
3.34
), thereby proving that

[\omega_{i}]^{\prime}_{\alpha,B_{2}(o),f_{i^{-1}},\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\leqslant C^{\prime}

for some constant
C^{\prime}
and all
i>0
if the claim is true, and hence proving Theorem
3.15
.

Proof:
We prove the claim by studying three cases:

3.5.5.
Case 1:

\varrho_{i}\to\infty
as
i\to\infty

\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,\mathscr{C}}\leqslant C
and
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}
implies that

\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\min_{\pi\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\pi\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})}\leqslant(C+1)\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\to 0,\quad i\to\infty,

showing the claim if
\varrho_{i}\to\infty
.

3.5.6.
Case 2:

\varrho_{i}
is bounded away from
0
and
\infty
, i.e., there exist
b,B>0
such that
b<\varrho_{i}<B
.

Subcase 2.
1)

0<a<\delta_{i}<A<\infty
for some
a,A>0
: The convergence
\tilde{\omega}_{i}\to\omega_{\infty}\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}
in
C^{1,\beta}(K)
for a compact set
K
containing all
B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})
implies that

\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\min_{\pi\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\pi\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})}\leqslant b^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\omega_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,K}\to 0.

Subcase 2.
2)

\delta_{i}\to 0
: In this case,
B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})
leaves every compact set in
\mathscr{C}
, and so we cannot use the convergence
\tilde{\omega}_{i}\to\omega_{\infty}
in
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
directly. For this part, we assume that
\varrho_{i}=1
at all times to ease notation. As
B_{1}(x_{i})
is not contained in a coordinate ball for
i
large enough, no element in
\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
other than 0 appear. Note that
\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})=1
, but this does not play a role here.

As
\tilde{\omega}_{i}\to\omega_{\infty}\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}
in
C_{\loc}^{1,\beta}(\mathscr{C})
, then for any small but fixed
\mu>0
and
i
large enough:

(3.35)

\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\omega_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[\mu,2]\times L}\leqslant\mu.

By the Hölder bound (
3.34
) and choosing
x=o
,
\varrho=2\mu
, and
\nu=\delta_{i}
in the seminorm (
3.34
), there exists
\psi_{i}\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}
such that

\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2\mu]\times L}\leqslant(2\mu)^{\alpha}.

The triangle inequality now implies that

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\omega_{\infty}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\mu,2\mu]\times L}

\displaystyle=\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\omega_{\infty}-\tilde{\omega}_{i}+\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\mu,2\mu]\times L}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\omega_{\infty}-\tilde{\omega}_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\mu,2\mu]\times L}+\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\mu,2\mu]\times L}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mu+(2\mu)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leqslant 3\mu^{\alpha}.

|\omega_{\infty}-\psi_{i}|_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}
is invariant under scaling, so it follows that

\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\omega_{\infty}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,(0,2]\times L}\leqslant 3\mu^{\alpha}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\min_{\pi\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\pi\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\setminus B_{\nu_{i}}(x_{i})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\omega_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2]\times L}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left(\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\omega_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2\mu]\times L}+\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\omega_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[2\mu,2]\times L}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\psi_{i}+\psi_{i}-\omega_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2\mu]\times L}+\mu

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\psi_{i}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2\mu]\times L}+\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\left\lVert\psi_{i}-\omega_{\infty}\right\rVert_{0,[\delta_{i},2\mu]\times L}+\mu

\displaystyle\leqslant(2\mu)^{\alpha}+3\mu^{\alpha}+\mu,

proving the claim in this case as
x_{i},\varrho_{i},\nu_{i}
realizes at least half of the seminorm (
3.33
).

Subcase 2.
3)

\delta_{i}\to\infty
. As
\dist_{\omega_{\mathscr{C}}}(x_{i},o)\to\infty
, and as the coordinate balls in
\mathfrak{U}
have increasing size moving away from the apex,
B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})
is always contained in a coordinate ball for
i
sufficiently large. Fix the associated holomorphic coordinates on any such coordinate ball, and apply translations such that
x_{i}=0
is always the center. In these coordinates,
\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
is by definition the set of constant
(1,1)
-forms. Recall that
\dist(x_{i},\partial(B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)))\to\infty
, so for every
i
, choose an increasing sequence of balls
B_{r_{i}}(x_{i})\subset B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)
containing
B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})
such that
r_{i}\to\infty
and
\dist_{\omega_{{\mathscr{C}}}}(B_{r_{i}}(x_{i}),\partial(B_{2(\epsilon_{i})^{-1}}(o)))\to\infty
. Also ensure
r_{i}\to\infty
so slowly such that
B_{r_{i}}(x_{i})
is always contained in a coordinate ball.

By the Liouville Theorem (Theorem
2.3
),
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}|_{B_{r_{i}}(x_{i})}\to A^{*}\omega_{\eucl}
in
C_{\loc}^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\tilde{\omega}_{i}|_{B_{r_{i}}(x_{i})}\to B^{*}\omega_{\eucl}
in
C_{\loc}^{1,\beta}(\mathbb{C}^{m})
for some
A,B\in\GL(m,\mathbb{C})
. Picking
\pi=\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
and
\eta=-A^{*}\omega_{\eucl}+B^{*}\omega_{\eucl}\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}
, then

\tilde{f}_{i}(\delta_{i})\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\min_{(\pi,\eta)\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\pi-\eta\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})}\leqslant b^{-\alpha}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\omega_{\mathscr{C}}+A^{*}\omega_{\eucl}-B^{*}\omega_{\eucl}\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})}\to 0

as
i\to\infty
, and the result follows.

3.5.7.
Case 3:

\varrho_{i}\to 0
. Investigating the relationship between
\delta_{i}
and
\varrho_{i}

In this case, we linearize the complex Monge-Ampère equation and apply Proposition
3.5
. This part of the proof also differs the most from the proof of Proposition
3.5
. Since we cannot directly cite a Liouville theorem as in Case 2, we first need to build up the necessary estimates.

The first thing that needs to be checked is the relationship between
\delta_{i}
and
\varrho_{i}
. Due to the regularity of the complex Monge-Ampère equation, we show that, under the condition
\varrho_{i}\to 0
, then

(3.36)

\delta_{i}\to 0,\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\delta_{i}}{\varrho_{i}}\leqslant A<\infty,

as
i\to\infty
and for some
A>0
.
\delta_{i}
therefore goes to zero, and goes to zero at least as fast as
\varrho_{i}
. To show this, we check two cases:

(1)

b\leqslant\delta_{i}
: The
C^{1,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
-regularity of
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
trivially implies a
C^{0,\beta}_{\loc}(\mathscr{C})
-bound on
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
for any
\beta<1
. As
\delta_{i}\geqslant b
by assumption, this estimate is uniform on
B_{\frac{b}{2}}(x_{i})
. Lemma
3.14
shows a similar bound for the primed seminorm:

[\tilde{\omega}_{i}]_{\beta,B_{\frac{b}{2}}(\hat{x}_{i}),\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}^{\prime}\leqslant D.

The same is true for any element in
\Sigma_{3C}^{2}
, especially
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
. Pick
\beta>\alpha
. As
B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})\subset B_{\frac{b}{2}}(x_{i})
for
i
big enough, it follows that:

(3.37)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varrho_{i}^{-\alpha}\min_{(\pi,\eta)\in\Sigma_{3C}^{2}\times\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\pi-\eta\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varrho_{i}^{\beta-\alpha}\varrho_{i}^{-\beta}\min_{\eta\in\Sigma_{\loc}^{2}}\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}_{i}-\omega_{\mathscr{C}}-\eta\right\rVert_{0,B_{\varrho_{i}}(x_{i})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varrho_{i}^{\beta-\alpha}D_{K}\to 0,

which leads to a contradiction.

(2)

\delta_{i}\to 0
, but
